, so
(\tilde{M},\tilde{N})n_{i}=(ln,dn)m_{i}
. Then for
P_{n}
and
F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n})=Fo\circ\varphi\circ\psi(Q_{i})
we have
en_{1,0}(P_{n})=(dn,ln)
,
en_{1,0}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n}))=(\tilde{N},\tilde{M})
and again
en_{1,0}(P_{n})m_{i}=en_{1,0}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n}))n_{i}
.

Then for
i<I

f_{0,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=\varepsilon_{i}y^{\tilde{M}+N\tilde{N}},\quad f_{0,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))=y^{ln+dnN}

(as
\alpha=1
). So,
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\Phi_{N,1}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n})))
and
f_{0,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n})))^{n_{i}}=\varepsilon_{i}^{n_{i}}f_{0,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))^{m_{i}}
.

Besides, since
F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n})
are of subrectangular type and
f_{1,0}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n}))
are monomials for
i=0,\ldots,I
by theorem
4.1
and remark
4.1
, we have for
i=0,\ldots,I

({Q}_{N,i})_{q}=0\quad\mbox{for $\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,i})-N<q<\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,i})$},

(6.2)

and
({Q}_{N,i})_{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,i})-N}=c\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,i})-N}
,
c\in K
, where
c
is a constant depending on
t
.

Step
2. From theorem
4.2
we know that for
i<I

en_{1,0}(Q_{i})=(l,d)\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})/d=(l_{2},d_{2})\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})/d_{2}.

So, for
i<I
we have
en_{1,0}(F_{i}(P_{n},Q_{n}))=(d_{2},l_{2})\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})/d_{2}
and therefore

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})=(d_{2}N+l_{2})\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})/d_{2}

(6.3)

(by theorem
4.1
we know that
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})
is divisible by
d_{2}
for
i<I
). Therefore, for
i<I
we have

SeqGCD_{i}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=SeqGCD_{i}(P,Q)(d_{2}N+l_{2})/d_{2}.

Now let’s calculate
SeqGCD_{I}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))
. By lemma
4.4
we know (see also the formulae above) that
en_{1,0}(Q_{I})=(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1,\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)
. So,
we have
en_{1,0}(F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n}))=(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1,\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)
and therefore

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})=N(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)+\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1.

Recall the definition of
\tilde{M}_{i}
from lemma
4.4
:
\tilde{M}_{i}=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})n_{i}-M_{i}
. So,

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{I-1})n_{I-1}(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})-M_{I-1}(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})+N+1=\\
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{I-1})n_{I-1}(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})-(d_{2}-l_{2})(k(M_{I-1}-1)-\frac{M_{I-1}}{d_{2}})

(6.4)

(here we put
N=(d_{2}-l_{2})k-1
for both cases of lemma). Therefore,

SeqGCD_{I}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=GCD(SeqGCD_{I-1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n})),\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I}))=\\
GCD(SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)(d_{2}N+l_{2})/d_{2},(d_{2}-l_{2})(k(M_{I-1}-1)-\frac{M_{I-1}}{d_{2}}))=\\
(d_{2}-l_{2})GCD(SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)(kd_{2}-1)/d_{2},(k(M_{I-1}-1)-\frac{M_{I-1}}{d_{2}}))=\\
(d_{2}-l_{2})SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)/d_{2},

where the last equality hold because
M_{I-1}/d_{2}
is divisible by
SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)/d_{2}
by theorem
4.1
and
k
is divisible by
SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)/d_{2}
by condition. Hence it is
>1
if conditions of item 1) hold. If conditions of item 2) hold, then this GCD is
1
for any
k
. Moreover, in this case

\sum_{i=0}^{I-1}(n_{i}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i+1}))=\sum_{i=0}^{I-2}(n_{i}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i+1}))(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})+\\
(n_{I-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I-1})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I}))=(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})(Dist(P,Q)+1)-N-1=\\
(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})(p+q)-N-1=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))+\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))-1-N,

(6.5)

which proves Item 2) of lemma. Note that all differences
(n_{i}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i+1})
are big if
k\gg 0
.

Step
3. At last, to prove Item 1), we need to calculate the polynomial
\hat{F}_{I+1}
, the operator
Q_{N,I+1}=\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n}))
and its order, distance and sequential GCD. Let’s take for simplicity of calculations
\hat{n}_{I}=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))=(ln+dnN)
, and
\hat{m}_{I}=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})
.

Recall that from Items 3.4. 7) of lemma
4.4
we know that
f_{0,1}(Q_{I})=\varepsilon(1+\alpha x)^{\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1}y^{\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1}
for some
\varepsilon\in K^{*}
,
Q_{I}=F_{I}(P,Q)
.
Since
Q_{I}
is of subrectangular type, we have therefore

f_{0,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=\varepsilon y^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N}.

So, we can find the constant
\hat{\varepsilon}_{I}
of
\hat{F}_{I+1}
38
38

38

from the equation
f_{0,1}(Q_{N,I})^{\hat{n}_{I}}-\hat{\varepsilon}_{I}f_{0,1}(\Phi_{N,\lambda}(P_{n}))^{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})}=0
:
\hat{\varepsilon}_{I}=\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}
, and so

\hat{F}_{I+1}(X,Y)=F_{I}(X,Y)^{(ln+dnN)}-\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}X^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N}.

By remark
3.1
we know that
F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{ln+dnN}
and
P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N}
are operators of subrectangular type. Moreover we have

en_{1,0}(F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{ln+dnN})=(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1,\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)\cdot(ln+dnN)\\
en_{1,0}(P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N})=(dn,ln)\cdot((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N).

From this we immediately get that

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{(ln+dnN)}=(l-d)nN<0\\
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits_{x}(P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits_{x}F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{(ln+dnN)}=(d-l)n>0,

that is

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n}))=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{\hat{n}_{I}}=(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)(ln+dnN)

and the highest monomials of the operators
F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{(ln+dnN)}
and
P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N}
belong to the
(N,1)
-top line of the operator
\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n})
, and this top line contains exactly these two vertices. Now direct calculation of the polynomial
f_{N,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n})))
gives

f_{N,1}(\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=(\varepsilon((x+y^{N})^{\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1}y^{\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1})^{(ln+dnN)}-\\
\varepsilon^{ln+dnN}(((x+y^{N})^{dn}y^{ln})^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N}=\\
\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}(x+y^{N})^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)(ln+dnN)}y^{ln((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)}\cdot(y^{N(d-l)n}-(x+y^{N})^{(d-l)n}).

Therefore the
(N,1)
-top line of the operator
\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n}))
contains no vertex on the
y
-axis, and contains the vertex
(1,(ln+dnN)((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)-N)
. Besides, the coefficient at this vertex is

c:=-\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}(d-l)n.

(6.6)

Step
4. Now we claim that

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=\\
(ln+dnN)((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)-N=(ln+dnN)\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})-N.

Since
f_{1,0}(F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{(ln+dnN)})
and
f_{1,0}(P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N})
are monomials, belonging to the
(N,1)
-top line above, the next monomials of these operators belonging to a lower
(N,1)
-line parallel to the top line, will be the second monomials
39
39

39

here we mean the next after the highest monomial of the polynomial
H_{1}(x)^{(ln+dnN)}
multiplied with
y^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)(ln+dnN)}
and, respectively, the next after the highest monomial of the polynomial
\tilde{g}(x)^{n((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)}
multiplied with
y^{ln((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)}

of

f_{0,1}(F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})^{(ln+dnN)})=H_{1}(x)^{(ln+dnN)}y^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)(ln+dnN)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\\
f_{0,1}(P_{n}^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N})=\tilde{g}(x)^{n((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)}y^{ln((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)},

where
H_{1},\tilde{g}
are polynomials from lemma
6.1
. Direct calculation of the sum of images of these monomials under automorphism
\Phi_{N,1}
gives

H_{1}(x)^{(ln+dnN)}=\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}(x^{(ln+dnN)(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)}+t(ln+dnN)(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)x^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)(ln+dnN-1)+\tilde{M}_{I-1}}+\ldots),

\tilde{g}(x)^{n((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)}=x^{dn((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)}+\\
tdn((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)x^{d(n((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)-1)+d-1}+\ldots,

whence

\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}(t(ln+dnN)(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)(x+y^{N})^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)(ln+dnN-1)+\tilde{M}_{I-1}}y^{(\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)(ln+dnN)}-\\
tdn((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)(x+y^{N})^{d(n((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)-1)+d-1}y^{ln((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)})=\\
-\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}(d-l)nty^{(ln+dnN)((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)-N}+\mbox{lower terms}.

So, we get the claim and moreover we have calculated the highest symbol

\sigma(\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n})))=-\varepsilon^{(ln+dnN)}(d-l)nt\partial^{(ln+dnN)((\tilde{M}_{I-1}l/d+1)+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)N)-N}.

The same observations show that the homogeneous components of
Q_{N,I+1}=\Phi_{N,1}(\hat{F}_{I+1}(P_{n},Q_{n}))
have the property

(Q_{N,I+1})_{q}=c_{q}\partial^{q},\quad c_{q}\in K\quad\mbox{for $\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,I+1})-N<q<\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,I+1})-1$, }

hence

Sdeg_{A}((Q_{N,I+1})_{q})=0\quad\mbox{ for $\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,I+1})-N<q<\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,I+1})-1$}

(and
Sdeg_{A}((Q_{N,I+1})_{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,I+1})-1})=1
).

Step
5. Now note that
SeqGCD_{I+1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=1
. Indeed, the number

SeqGCD_{I}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))
divides
(d_{2}-l_{2})SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)
, because
(kd_{2}-1)
is coprime with
(k(M_{I-1}-1)-M_{I-1}/d_{2})
. And also this number divides
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})
. Since

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I+1})=(ln+dnN)(\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I}))-N=(ln+dnN)(\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I}))-(d_{2}-l_{2})k+1

and
k
is divisible by
SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)
, we get

SeqGCD_{I+1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=GCD(SeqGCD_{I}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n})),\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I+1}))=1.

Moreover,

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I+1})=1\quad\mbox{mod $SeqGCD_{I}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))$}.

Step
6. At last, the distance is

Dist(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=\sum_{i=0}^{I-1}(n_{i}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i+1}))+(\hat{n}_{I}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,I+1}))=\\
(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})(Dist(P,Q)+1)-1=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))+\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))-1.

∎

6.2
Some explicit calculations

In this subsection we provide calculations of coefficients of the Puiseux series corresponding to the sequence of commuting differential operators mentioned at the beginning of this section.

Step
1. Consider the operators
P_{n},Q_{n},\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n})
from lemma
6.2
. Note that they are monic as differential operators and as operators in
\hat{D}_{1}^{sym}
,

\sigma(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))=\partial^{ln+dnN},\quad\sigma(\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=\partial^{lm+dmN},

and

(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))_{q}=0\quad\mbox{ for \quad}ln+dnN>q>ln+ndN-N

(cf. the observations at the end of Step 1 of the proof lemma
6.2
), i.e.
\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n})
is normalized, and analogously

(\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))_{q}=0\quad\mbox{ for \quad}lm+dmN>q>lm+dmN-N.

Besides,
(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))_{ln+ndN-N}=\tilde{c}_{1}\partial^{ln+ndN-N}
,
(\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))_{lm+dmN-N}=\tilde{c}_{2}\partial^{lm+dmN-N}
, where
\tilde{c}_{1},\tilde{c}_{2}\in K
depend on
t
.

Step
2. Let
S
be a Schur operator from proposition
2.4
for
\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n})
:
S^{-1}\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n})S=\partial^{ln+dnN}
. Note that
Sdeg_{A}(S_{q})=0
for
q>-N
(because otherwise
(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))_{q}\neq 0
). Therefore, such
S_{q}\in C(\partial^{ln+dnN})
and we can find, multiplying with an appropriate operator from the centralizer, an operator
S
with
S_{q}=0
for
q=-1,\ldots,-N+1
.

Consider now the operators
\tilde{Q}_{N,i}:=S^{-1}Q_{N,i}S
. Then

(\tilde{Q}_{N,i})_{q}=(Q_{N,i})_{q}\in K\quad\mbox{ for $q>\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,i})-N$ for all $i\leq I+1$}.

In view of lemma
6.2
, the pair
(\partial^{ln+dnN},\tilde{Q}_{N,0})
is a normal string pair of order
(ln+dnN,lm+dmN)
. Let

(\partial^{ln+dnN},\tilde{Q}_{N,0}^{\prime}):=(\partial^{ln+dnN},\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-ln-dnN})

be the corresponding normal pair (see theorem
5.1
and corollary
5.1
).

Step
3. For simplicity of further calculations let’s take a sequence of polynomials
F_{i}(X,Y)
for initial operators
P,Q
from section
6.1
for which
n_{i}=p
for all
i
(see theorem
4.1
for notation). To fix notation, let
(\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{i},\epsilon_{i})
,
i=0,\ldots,I
be the corresponding sequence for the pair
P,Q
. Then by lemma
6.2
there exists similar sequence
(\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,i},\epsilon_{i})
also for the pair
\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n})
(with the same
\epsilon_{i}
for
i=0,\ldots,I
). Below we’ll continue to use notations from lemma
6.2
and assume
N>1
.

Let
P_{N,n}^{\prime},Q_{N,n}^{\prime}
be the normalised pair of commuting operators corresponding to the string pair
\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n})
according to corollary
5.1
. Denote by
Q_{N,i}^{\prime}:=F_{i}(P_{N,n}^{\prime},Q_{N,n}^{\prime})
the corresponding sequence of differential operators (by corollary
5.1
this is the same sequence of polynomials
F_{i}
as for
\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n})
from lemma
6.2
), and by
\tilde{Q}_{N,i}^{\prime}:=F_{i}(\tilde{P}_{N,n}^{\prime},\tilde{Q}_{N,n}^{\prime})
the sequence of normal forms of
Q_{N,i}^{\prime}
with respect to
P_{N,n}^{\prime}
.

Denote by
X_{N,n}\in K((\tilde{D}^{-1}))
the Puiseux series obtained from
\tilde{Q}_{N,0}^{\prime}=\tilde{Q}_{N,n}^{\prime}
by conjugation with the auxiliary operator
\tilde{S}
from lemma
5.1
(so,
X_{N,n}=\tilde{S}^{-1}\hat{\Phi}(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}^{\prime})\tilde{S}
is a solution of the BC equation
f(\tilde{D}^{ln+Ndn},X)=0
giving the algebraic relation between
P_{N,n}^{\prime},Q_{N,n}^{\prime}
, cf. remark
2.9
), and by
X
the corresponding Puiseux series obtained from
\tilde{Q}^{\prime}
(a normal form of
Q^{\prime}
with respect to
P^{\prime}
, where
P^{\prime},Q^{\prime}
is the normalised pair of commuting operators corresponding to the string pair
P,Q
). Denote by
X_{i}:=F_{i}(\tilde{D}^{dn},X)\in K((\tilde{D}^{-1}))

X_{N,i}:=F_{i}(\tilde{D}^{ln+Ndn},X_{N,n})\in K((\tilde{D}^{-1}))
the corresponding sequence of series. To fix notation let

X=\tilde{D}^{dm}+\sum_{j<dm}c_{j}\tilde{D}^{j},\quad X_{i}=\epsilon_{i}\tilde{D}^{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{i}}+\sum_{j<\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{i}}c_{i;j}\tilde{D}^{j};

X_{N,n}=\tilde{D}^{dmN+lm}+\sum_{j<dmN+lm}c_{n,j}\tilde{D}^{j},\quad X_{N,i}=\epsilon_{i}\tilde{D}^{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,i}}+\sum_{j<\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,i}}c_{N,i;j}\tilde{D}^{j}.

All series
X_{N,n},X_{N,i}
have
distinguished coefficients
– these are coefficients of homogeneous terms corresponding to the beginning of the ”tail” of operators
\tilde{Q}_{N,i}
. For
X_{N,n}
such coefficient is
c_{n,-dnN-ln}
, and for arbitrary
X_{N,k}
such coefficient is
c_{N,k;r(k)}
where

r(k)=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,k}-((N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})(dn+dm)-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}(p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i+1})))

(to explain this formula, lets note that at each step of the head-chopping process the distance between a new head and the beginning of the tail is shortened by the amount
(p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i+1}))
, and the initial distance is
(N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})(dn+dm)=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))+\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))
).

By remark
5.3
we know that
c_{n,-dnN-ln}=0
. From this we can immediately deduce that all distinguished coefficients
c_{N,k;r(k)}
for
k\leq I
are polynomial expressions in
\epsilon_{i}
,
i<I
with integer coefficients
not depending on
N
:

Lemma 6.3
.

In the notations above the distinguished coefficients
c_{N,k;r(k)}
for
k\leq I
are polynomial expressions in
\epsilon_{i}
,
i<I
with integer coefficients not depending on
N
(i.e. they are the same for any
N
).

Proof.

By lemma
6.2
(cf. formula (
6.3
)) we have

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,i}=(d_{2}N+l_{2})\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})/d_{2}\quad\mbox{ for $i<I$},

and
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,i}=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,i}^{\prime}
,
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i})=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i}^{\prime})
(by corollary
5.1
the sequences coincide both for
Q_{N,n}
,
Q_{N,n}^{\prime}
and for
Q
,
Q^{\prime}
).
Therefore, all non-zero coefficients
c_{n,i}
for
i>-dnN-ln+N+1
(cf. equation (
6.5
)) must have indices
i
divisible by
(d_{2}N+l_{2})
(cf. remark
5.2
). Moreover,

c_{n,(d_{2}N+l_{2})i}=c_{i}\quad\mbox{for $i>-dn+1$},

because the polynomials
F_{i}
,
i\leq I
are the same for both pairs of operators (
P^{\prime},Q^{\prime}
and
P_{N,n}^{\prime},Q_{N,n}^{\prime}
) and therefore the expressions for these coefficients in terms of
\epsilon_{i}
coincide.
40
40

40

By the same reason
c_{-dn+1}=c_{n,-dnN-ln+N+1}
, though we’ll not need this equality below.

Now our claim can be proved by induction on
k
. For
k=0
the distinguished coefficients
c_{N,0;r(0)}
are zero by remark
5.3
.

For
k\leq I
the expressions for
c_{N,k;r(k)}
in terms of coefficients
c_{n,i}
are
polynomials in

c_{n,(d_{2}N+l_{2})i}
for
i>-dn+1
and are the same for all
N
(and coincide with the expression for the distinguished coefficients of
X_{i}
in terms of
c_{i}
for
i>-dn+1
). To prove it, we need to find expression for
c_{N,k;r(k)}
in terms of coefficients of the series
X_{N,k-1}
. By induction hypothesis, the expressions for coefficients

c_{N,k-1;j}
for

j>\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,k-1}-((N+\frac{l_{2}}{d_{2}})(dn+dm)-\sum_{i=0}^{k-2}(p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i+1})))+N+1

(6.7)

in terms of coefficients
c_{n,(d_{2}N+l_{2})i}
for
i>-dn+1
are the same for all
N
(the explanation of this formula is analogous to the formula for
r(k)
above; in view of equation (
6.5
) the distance between the distinguished coefficient
c_{N,I,r(I)}=0
and the head of
Q_{N,I}
is
N+1
, so we need to take into account only coefficients of bigger distances from the distinguished coefficients in all series
X_{N,i}
).

The expression for
c_{N,k;r(k)}
in terms of coefficients of the series
X_{N,k-1}
is the homogeneous component of the series
X_{N,k-1}^{p}
of order
r(k)
– the sum of products of homogeneous components of the series
X_{N,k-1}
, let’s call such products as monomials for short. If
k<I
, in view of formula (
6.3
), the distance between the head of
X_{N,k-1}
and the next non-zero homogeneous component is

p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,k-1}-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,k}=(d_{2}N+l_{2})(p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{k-1}-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{k})>N+1,

therefore any non-zero monomial of the series
X_{N,k-1}^{p}
of order
r(k)
will consist only of products of homogeneous components of orders
j
satisfying inequality (
6.7
).

If
k=I
, by Step 2 of the proof of lemma
6.2
(formula (
6.3
)) we have

p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,k-1}-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,k}=(d_{2}-l_{2})(k(M_{I-1}-1)-\frac{M_{I-1}}{d_{2}}),

and this number is less than
N+1=(d_{2}-l_{2})k
only if
M_{I-1}=d_{2}=2
. In this case
l_{2}=1
, an so the case 2) of lemma
6.2
holds, and

p\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I-1}-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}=N.

In this case a non-zero monomial of the series
X_{N,I-1}^{p}
of order
r(I)
can contain a homogeneous components of order
j
not satisfying inequality (
6.7
) only if the homogeneous component of order
r(I-1)+N
is not equal to zero. But this is impossible: by formulae (
6.5
), (
6.2
) we have
(Q_{N,I})_{r(I)+N}=(Q_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-1}=0
. Clearly,
r(I)+N,r(I-1)+N\notin(2N+1){\mbox{\dbl Z}}
(as all numbers
r(k)
are divisible by
d_{2}N+l_{2}
by lemma
6.2
), so by the observations from remark
5.2
we must have
c_{N,I-1;r(I-1)+N}=0
. So, in this case again any non-zero monomial of the series
X_{N,k-1}^{p}
of order
r(k)
will consist only of products of homogeneous components of orders
j
satisfying inequality (
6.7
), and we are done.
∎

Step
4. The next step in our proof is to calculate the distinguished coefficient
c_{N,I;r(I)}
.
To provide this calculation, we need to calculate first the corresponding homogeneous component of order
r(I)
of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}
, then calculate the body-tail decomposition for this component (this component is the beginning of the tail, so the calculation is not difficult), and finally calculate the result of conjugation by the auxiliary operator
\tilde{S}
. In fact, we will not even need to calculate
\tilde{S}
, because the result of conjugation for this component will be just the averaging of components of its vector form. We’ll see that the result of direct calculation of
c_{N,I;r(I)}
will depend on
N
, thus contradicting lemma
6.3
.

Let’s calculate the homogeneous component of order
r(I)
of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}
– this is the component
(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}
by lemma
6.2
, formula (
6.5
). To calculate it, we need to find first two non-zero homogeneous components
S_{-N},S_{-N-1}
of the Schur operator
S
from Step 2. As usual,
S
can be represented as a product of elementary operators
\bar{S}_{i}:=1+s_{i}\int^{-i}
, and here
i\leq-N
. Note that conjugation by
\bar{S}_{-N}
does not affect the homogeneous component of order
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1
, so we need to find only the operator
\bar{S}_{-N-1}
and the result of conjugation by it.

Note that we have

(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))_{Ndn+ln-N-1}=(dn\Gamma_{1}+a)\partial^{Ndn+ln-N-1},\quad\\
(Q_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(Q_{N,I})-N-1}=\epsilon_{I}((\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)\Gamma_{1}+b)\partial^{N\tilde{M}_{I-1}+\tilde{M}_{I-1}l_{2}/d_{2}},

where
a
is a coefficient of the operator
P_{n}
and
b
is a coefficient of the operator
Q_{n,I}=F_{I}(P_{n},Q_{n})
which are therefore do not depend on
N
. So,
\bar{S}_{-N-1}
should be of the shape (cf. proposition
2.4
)

\bar{S}_{-N-1}=1+(\gamma_{2}\Gamma_{2}+\gamma_{1}\Gamma_{1}+u)\int^{N+1},

where
\Phi(u)\in K^{\oplus Ndn+ln}
and
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in K
(
u
can be found with the help of the condition
\bar{S}_{-N-1}
is totally free of
B_{j}
). From the formula above and commutation relations of lemma
2.2
we get

\bar{S}_{-N-1}^{-1}\bar{S}_{-N}^{-1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))\bar{S}_{-N}\bar{S}_{-N-1}=\partial^{Ndn+ln}+\\
(\gamma_{2}(\Gamma_{1}+ln+Ndn)^{2}+\gamma_{1}(\Gamma_{1}+ln+Ndn)-\gamma_{2}\Gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}\Gamma_{1}+dn\Gamma_{1}+a)\partial^{Ndn+ln-N-1}+\ldots,

where from we get

\gamma_{2}=-\frac{d_{2}}{2(Nd_{2}+l_{2})},\quad\gamma_{1}=-\frac{a}{ln+Ndn}+\frac{dn}{2}.

Now we can calculate
(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}
. Since
\sigma(Q_{N,I})=\epsilon_{I}\partial^{N(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)+\tilde{M}_{I-1}l_{2}/d_{2}+1}
, we get after conjugation, similarly to the formula above,

(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}=\epsilon_{I}(\gamma_{2}(\Gamma_{1}+N(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)+\tilde{M}_{I-1}l_{2}/d_{2}+1)^{2}+\gamma_{1}(\Gamma_{1}+N(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)+\tilde{M}_{I-1}l_{2}/d_{2}+1)+\\
\sigma^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}}(u)-\gamma_{2}\Gamma_{2}-\gamma_{1}\Gamma_{1}-u+(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)\Gamma_{1}+b)\partial^{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}=\\
\epsilon_{I}(-\frac{d-l}{dN+l}\Gamma_{1}+(\sigma^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}}(u)-u)+A\frac{N^{2}}{Nd+l}+B\frac{N}{Nd+l}+C\frac{1}{Nd+l}+b),

(6.8)

where

A=\frac{1}{2}d(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)(dn-\tilde{M}_{I-1}-1),

B=\frac{1}{2}\left(-\frac{2(\tilde{M}_{I-1}+1)(a+l\tilde{M}_{I-1}n)}{n}+d^{2}n+d(2\tilde{M}_{I-1}(ln-1)+ln-2)\right),

C=-\frac{(d+l\tilde{M}_{I-1})(2a+n(d(-l)n+d+l\tilde{M}_{I-1}))}{2dn}.

Note that
A\neq 0
, because
\tilde{M}_{I-1}=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{I-1})nd-M_{I-1}
,
M_{I-1}<p+q=d(n+m)
and
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{I-1})\geq dm
is a polynomial in
p
of order
I-1
(see lemma
4.4
).

Remark 6.2
.

Since
(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}))_{ln+ndN-N}=\tilde{c}_{1}\partial^{ln+ndN-N}
, where
\tilde{c}_{1}
is a constant, the coefficient
s_{-N}
of the operator
\bar{S}_{-N}
is a linear polynomial in
\Gamma_{1}
, hence
Sdeg_{A}((\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N})=0
and
(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N}\in C(\partial^{ln+ndN})
. So, the component
(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}
is the first component with
Sdeg_{A}>0
, i.e. it is the beginning of the tail for the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}
.

Step
5. (body-tail decomposition) Put

\tilde{d}:=SeqGCD_{I-1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))=SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)(d_{2}N+l_{2})/d_{2},\quad\tilde{p}:=ln+dnN.

Since
SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)/d_{2}
divides
GCD(l,d)
(by theorem
4.1
), we can decompose
\tilde{p}
as the product

\tilde{p}=\tilde{d}\tilde{d}_{1}n,

where
GCD(l,d)=\tilde{d}_{1}SeqGCD_{I-1}(P,Q)/d_{2}
.

We need to find a relation between operators
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}^{\prime}={F}_{I}(\partial^{\tilde{p}},\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})
and
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}={F}_{I}(\partial^{\tilde{p}},\tilde{Q}_{N,0})
.

From previous step we know the shape of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}
:

\tilde{Q}_{N,I}=\epsilon_{I}\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})}+\tilde{q}\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})-N}+(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}+\ldots,\quad Sdeg_{A}(\tilde{q})=0.

Let’s introduce the following terminology. Each operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,i}
can be decomposed as a sum of a ”body” from the centralizer
C(\partial^{\tilde{p}})
and a ”tail”, which is a sum of products of the ”bodies” and the ”tails”
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}}
. Note that any non-zero homogeneous component of this ”tail” has positive
Sdeg_{A}
. This can be shown by induction. For the operator
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})
the body is

(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})
and the tail is
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}}
, and its form is determined by lemma
4.1
. Then

\tilde{Q}_{N,1}=(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})^{n_{0}}-\epsilon_{0}^{n_{0}}\partial^{\tilde{p}m_{0}}=(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})^{n_{0}}-\epsilon_{0}^{n_{0}}\partial^{\tilde{p}m_{0}}+\mbox{tail},

where the body is
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})^{n_{0}}-\epsilon_{0}^{n_{0}}\partial^{\tilde{p}m_{0}}
and the tail consists of sum of ”monomials” of the shape

(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})^{k_{1}}(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}}(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})^{k_{2}}(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}}\ldots.

From lemma
4.1
we know the shape of
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}}
:
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}}=(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+u)\int^{\tilde{p}}
, where
u\in C(\partial^{\tilde{p}})
. Then by commutation relations from lemma
2.2
we can rewrite these ”monomials” in the form

(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})^{\sum k_{i}}(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+u_{1})\int^{\tilde{p}}\ldots(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+u_{r})\int^{\tilde{p}},\quad u_{i}\in C(\partial^{\tilde{p}}).

The product of terms containing
\Gamma_{1}
has
Sdeg_{A}
equal to
r
(the highest term of this HCP is just the product of the highest terms of each factor), and therefore each non-zero homogeneous component of the expression above has
Sdeg_{A}
equal to
r
. Moreover, all these non-zero homogeneous components arise from
non-zero homogeneous components of
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0}-(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})_{-\tilde{p}})^{\sum k_{i}}
– the power of the body of the operator
(\tilde{Q}_{N,0})
.

By induction,

\tilde{Q}_{N,i+1}=(body)_{i}^{n_{i}}-\epsilon_{i}^{n_{i}}\partial^{\tilde{p}m_{i}}+\mbox{tail},

(6.9)

where the tail consist of a sum of ”monomials” of
Sdeg_{A}>0
(one ”monomial” for each order), and the ”monomial” of
Sdeg_{A}=r
has the shape

(body)_{i}^{k_{1}}(body)_{i-1}^{l_{1}}\ldots(body)_{i}^{k_{2}}(body)_{i-1}^{l_{2}}\ldots(body)_{j}^{l_{j}}(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+u_{1})\int^{\tilde{p}}\ldots(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+u_{r})\int^{\tilde{p}}

(we’ll call (
6.9
) as a ”body-tail” decomposition).

Step
6. Returning to the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}
, we see that the homogeneous component
(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}

includes a summand from the tail. This summand from the tail is the highest order homogeneous component of the ”monomial” above of
Sdeg_{A}=1
, and it can be calculated:

Lemma 6.4
.

The tail component of the term
(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})_{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}-N-1}
is

\epsilon_{I}(-dn+ln)(\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}-\tilde{u}+c_{0})\partial^{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits({Q}_{N,I})-1-N},

where

i)
\tilde{u}\in C(\partial^{\tilde{p}})
is a ”
\tilde{d}
-symmetrization” of the corresponding term in the tail given by lemma
4.1
, which can be effectively written in the vector form:

\Phi(\tilde{u})=(a_{0},\ldots,a_{\tilde{p}-1}),

where
a_{i}=a_{i+\tilde{d}}=a_{i+2\tilde{d}}=\ldots
, and for
i=0,\ldots,\tilde{d}-1

a_{i}=(\frac{i}{\tilde{p}}+\frac{i+\tilde{d}}{\tilde{p}}+\ldots+\frac{\tilde{d}(n\tilde{d}_{1}-1)+i}{\tilde{p}})/(n\tilde{d}_{1})=\frac{n\tilde{d}_{1}-1}{2n\tilde{d}_{1}}+\frac{i}{\tilde{p}};

ii)
c_{0}\in K
is given by the formula

c_{0}=(\sum_{i=0}^{I-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i}))\cdot\frac{(p-1)}{2p}

(recall that we put all
n_{i}=p=nd
in Step 3).

Proof.

The highest coefficient of the tail component we know from formula (
6.8
).
To find other coefficients let’s use induction. The tail component of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,0}
in vector form is

(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+(0,1/\tilde{p},\ldots,(\tilde{p}-1)/\tilde{p}))\tilde{D}^{-\tilde{p}}
according to lemma
4.1
. The tail component of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,1}
in vector form is

\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}\tilde{D}^{i\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,0})}(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}+(0,1/\tilde{p},\ldots,(\tilde{p}-1)/\tilde{p})\tilde{D}^{-\tilde{p}+(p-1-i)\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,0})}=\\
\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}(-(\Gamma_{1}+i\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,0}))/\tilde{p}+\sigma^{-i\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,0})}((0,1/\tilde{p},\ldots,(\tilde{p}-1)/\tilde{p})))=\\
p(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}-\frac{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,0})}{\tilde{p}}\cdot\frac{(p-1)}{2}+\tilde{u}_{1})\tilde{D}^{-\tilde{p}+(p-1)\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,0})},

where
\tilde{u}_{1}
is the
GCD(\tilde{p},\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,0})
-symmetrization of
(0,1/\tilde{p},\ldots,(\tilde{p}-1)/\tilde{p})
. By induction, the tail component of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,i}
in vector form is

\epsilon_{1}^{p-1}\cdots\epsilon_{i-1}^{p-1}p^{i}(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}-\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})}{\tilde{p}}\cdot\frac{(p-1)}{2}+\tilde{u}_{i})\tilde{D}^{-\tilde{p}+(p-1)\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})},

where
\tilde{u}_{i}
is the
SeqGCD_{i-1}(\Phi_{N,1}(P_{n}),\Phi_{N,1}(Q_{n}))
-symmetrization of
(0,1/\tilde{p},\ldots,(\tilde{p}-1)/\tilde{p})
. Then the tail component of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,i+1}
in vector form is

\epsilon_{1}^{p-1}\cdots\epsilon_{i-1}^{p-1}\epsilon_{i}^{p-1}p^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{p-1}\tilde{D}^{k\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})}(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}-\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})}{\tilde{p}}\cdot\frac{(p-1)}{2}\\
+\tilde{u}_{i})\tilde{D}^{-\tilde{p}+(p-1)\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})+(p-1-k)\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,i})}=\\
\epsilon_{1}^{p-1}\cdots\epsilon_{i-1}^{p-1}\epsilon_{i}^{p-1}p^{i+1}(-\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}-\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{i}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})}{\tilde{p}}\cdot\frac{(p-1)}{2}+\tilde{u}_{i+1})\tilde{D}^{-\tilde{p}+(p-1)\sum_{j=0}^{i}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})},

whence we get the claim of lemma with

\epsilon_{1}^{p-1}\cdots\epsilon_{I-1}^{p-1}p^{I}=\epsilon_{I}(dn-ln)

by formula (
6.8
), and the formula for
c_{0}
holds because
\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{N,j})=(d_{2}N+l_{2})\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{j})/d_{2}
.
∎

So, the ”body-tail” decomposition of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}
looks like

\tilde{Q}_{N,I}=(\epsilon_{I}\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})}+\tilde{q}\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})-N}+\epsilon_{I}((-dn+ln)(\tilde{u}-c_{0})+(\sigma^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}}(u)-u)+\\
A\frac{N^{2}}{Nd+l}+B\frac{N}{Nd+l}+C\frac{1}{Nd+l}+b)\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})-1-N}+\ldots)+\\
\epsilon_{I}(-dn+ln)(\Gamma_{1}/\tilde{p}-\tilde{u}+c_{0})\partial^{\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits({Q}_{N,I})-1-N}+\ldots

Hence, first terms of the operator
\tilde{Q}_{N,I}^{\prime}
are

\tilde{Q}_{N,I}^{\prime}=\epsilon_{I}\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})}+\tilde{q}\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})-N}+\epsilon_{I}((-dn+ln)(\tilde{u}-c_{0})+(\sigma^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}}(u)-u)+\\
A\frac{N^{2}}{Nd+l}+B\frac{N}{Nd+l}+C\frac{1}{Nd+l}+b)\partial^{\mathop{\rm\bf ord}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I})-1-N}+\ldots

Step
7. Now we can calculate the distinguished coefficient
c_{N,I;r(I)}
. To find it, we need to find the result of conjugation of
\hat{\Phi}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I}^{\prime})
by the auxiliary operator
\tilde{S}
from lemma
5.1
. According to the proof of this lemma,
\tilde{S}
can be represented as a product of elementary operators
\tilde{S}_{i}=1+s_{i}\tilde{D}^{-i}
. In our case obviously

i\geq N
. Note that conjugation by
\bar{S}_{N}
does not affect the homogeneous component of the operator
\hat{\Phi}(\tilde{Q}_{N,I}^{\prime})
of order
\mathop{\rm\bf ord}\tilde{Q}_{N,I}-N-1
, so we need to find only to find the result of conjugation by the auxiliary operator
\bar{S}_{N+1}
. This is just the averaging of components of its vector components.

The averaging of the vector components of the term
(\sigma^{-\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits Q_{N,I}}(u)-u)
is obviously zero. So, we need only to calculate the averaging of
\tilde{u}
from lemma
6.4
. It is equal to

\frac{1}{\tilde{d}}(\sum_{i=0}^{\tilde{d}-1}a_{i})=\frac{n\tilde{d}_{1}-1}{2n\tilde{d}_{1}}+\frac{(\tilde{d}-1)}{2\tilde{p}}

Combining all terms together, we get

c_{N,I;r(I)}=\epsilon_{I}((-dn+ln)(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2n(Nd+l)}-(\sum_{i=0}^{I-1}\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(Q_{i}))\cdot\frac{(p-1)}{2p})+A\frac{N^{2}}{Nd+l}+B\frac{N}{Nd+l}+C\frac{1}{Nd+l}+b).

It remains to note that this expression non-trivially depends on
N
(as
A\neq 0
) – a contradiction with lemma
6.3
.

So, there are no DC-pairs, and
\mathop{\rm End}(A_{1})\backslash 0=\mathop{\rm Aut}(A_{1})
.
